It was too much, and I don’t usually speak to people like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how you felt in the moment before you snapped at the volunteer? Were you feeling particularly overwhelmed, frustrated, or upset about something else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I guess I was wound up. It was just a crooked tablecloth, but I snapped at him like it was a big thing. I don’t usually talk to people that way.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me about how you usually get along with others—especially in situations where you're working together or in close proximity, like with volunteers or coworkers?\n\nPatient: I usually get along all right with people. I’m not much for fussing with anyone. That’s why this bothered me… I snapped at one of the younger volunteers over a crooked tablecloth. It wasn’t like me to talk that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had ongoing problems with your temper or difficulty getting along with others, even before this incident with the volunteer?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I’ve had my moments like anybody, but I haven’t had trouble getting along with people. That’s why this shook me some — blowing up at that young volunteer over a crooked tablecloth. I don’t usually talk to people like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if you’ve ever had sudden outbursts that came out of nowhere, where you felt like you lost control completely, even for a short time?\n\nPatient: Not completely, no… not like I was out of control. Just that time with the young volunteer—I raised my voice over a crooked tablecloth. It caught me off guard, because I don’t usually speak to people that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself needing things to be done a certain way, like being very particular about how tasks are completed or how things are arranged?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I like things neat enough, but I wouldn’t say I’m usually particular like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you tend to avoid social situations or feel uncomfortable around others, even if you don’t lose your temper?\n\nPatient: Lately, yes… a bit. I stopped going to card nights. I just don’t feel quite steady around people right now. And I haven’t kept up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event like I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Has this incident with the volunteer affected your ability to do your usual activities or interact with others in your community?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it has. I haven’t really kept up with the Día de los Muertos planning like I usually would. And I stopped going to card nights for now. I just don’t feel quite steady around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried most days, even when there isn’t a specific reason for it?\n\nPatient: Not most days, no. I don’t usually talk to people like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if you’ve ever had sudden outbursts that came out of nowhere, where you felt like you lost control completely, even for a short time?\n\nPatient: Not completely, no… not like I was out of control. Just that time with the young volunteer—I raised my voice over a crooked tablecloth. It caught me off guard, because I don’t usually speak to people that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself needing things to be done a certain way, like being very particular about how tasks are completed or how things are arranged?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I like things neat enough, but I wouldn’t say I’m usually particular like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you tend to avoid social situations or feel uncomfortable around others, even if you don’t lose your temper?\n\nPatient: Lately, yes… a bit. I stopped going to card nights. I just don’t feel quite steady around people right now. And I haven’t kept up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event like I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Has this incident with the volunteer affected your ability to do your usual activities or interact with others in your community?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it has. I haven’t really kept up with the Día de los Muertos planning like I usually would. And I stopped going to card nights for now. I just don’t feel quite steady around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried most days, even when there isn’t a specific reason for it?\n\nPatient: Not most days, no. I get uneasy sometimes, but I wouldn’t say it’s just there all the time for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you’re more irritable or quick to anger these days, even in situations that wouldn’t have bothered you before?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose so. That’s really what brought me in. I snapped at one of the younger volunteers over a tablecloth being crooked. It wasn’t… it wasn’t like me to talk to someone that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your irritability or anger lasts for most of the day, even when things are going normally?\n\nPatient: No… not most of the day. It’s more that I had that one blowup with the young volunteer, over something stupid like a crooked tablecloth. That’s what worried me. I don’t usually talk to people that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, even when things aren’t stressful?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I have my moments, but I wouldn’t say I’m hopeless or that I’ve stopped caring about things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself being more irritable or easily annoyed in general, even when you’re not in situations that involve other people or stress?\n\nPatient: Not really in general, no. It’s more… what I mentioned, that I blew up at that young volunteer over the tablecloth. That’s the thing that bothered me, because I don’t usually speak to people like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling more irritable or angry in general, even when you're not in situations that would usually upset you?\n\nPatient: No… not just out of nowhere. It was mostly that incident I already mentioned, with the young volunteer and the crooked tablecloth. I snapped at them, and that’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty getting close to people or forming close relationships, or do you often feel like you don't fit in with others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I’ve never been the most outgoing, but I wouldn’t say I can’t get close to people or that I don’t fit in.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever acted on sudden impulses that were harmful or out of character for you, even if it wasn’t directed at another person?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so.
